# Data Plan

## Context provenance
- `Can you make a digital binder of every Lorcana card that I own? And the ones that I'm missing can you I don't know highlight it in like you know like a yellow highlight?` (verbatim user request; full-set binder, owned vs missing in one layout)
- `Or maybe the cards that I'm missing, maybe not yellow, maybe like a like a ghostly figure of the card that I'm missing.` (verbatim user request, accepted over the yellow highlight; missing cards render as ghosted/faded versions of the real card art)
- `Can you include that promo binder as well?` + `Can can you make the binders four by three?` (verbatim user request; promo section included, 12 pockets per page in a 4x3 grid like his Vault X binders)
- `You could take away the shareable. I wouldn't even want to share it. Yeah, but load the load the images in for me for my private.` (verbatim user request; private artifact, real card artwork loaded live — this supersedes the earlier shareable static binder that had to use designed card faces with no art)
- `It would be nice to be able to flip through the cards without having to go get them off the shelf` (verbatim user statement; browsing without pulling physical binders is the core interaction)
- `Jarvis binder looks amazing I love how searchable it is let's not flip through pages let's just have it as one constant four by all the way down does that make sense?` (verbatim user request, Nov 2 2026; supersedes page-flipping — each section is now one continuous scroll, 4 cards across all the way down, no pager; search jumps by scrolling to the card)
- Standing context (MEMORY.md): his master set = regular + cold foil + Epics; promo binder ordered by set and card number; latest Shiny export Nov 1, 2026 (3,705 rows, 9,500 cards, $4,418.05) predates arrival of the Hyperia City and Attack of the Vine TCGplayer orders, so ownership is labeled 'as of your Nov 1 Shiny export' and is editable in the UI.
- Standing context (USER.md): Anthony is a fan of dark themes and dislikes bright backgrounds — binder uses a deep ink-dark theme. **Theme change (Nov 2, 2026)**: Anthony asked to replace the binder's yellow/gold theme with something more modern and elegant — accents, highlights, and headers now use a jade-teal palette (#45a58e accent, #9ed3c3 pale-jade highlight) on slightly cooler charcoal surfaces (#0a0f11 bg, #131a1d surface); the --gold CSS variable names are kept but now hold jade values. Foil shimmer and ghosted missing-card treatments are unchanged.

## Tested sources
### Anthony's Shiny collection export (ownership truth)
**Used by**: seedBinder (ownership join), card detail values
**Test command**: `python3 csv read of ~/workspace/user/files/ShinyExport-6533e14c87234a7792ff9a660a4c26b6_2_tm5t.csv`
**Sample output**: 3,705 rows, 9,500 cards; columns include product_name, set_name, discriminator (e.g. `17/207`, promo `34/P2`, `3/RPH`, `14/PD1`), rarity (`Rare`, `Rare Cold Foil`, `Epic Foil`, `Promo Foil`, `Enchanted`...), quantity, value_per_unit, tcgplayer_id. Sets: all 14 main sets, Disney Lorcana Promos (71 printings), D23 Promos (3), Illumineer's Quest Deep Trouble + Palace Heist.
**Processing**: aggregate rows by tcgplayer_id -> per-card qty total + finish breakdown (regular / cold foil / epic foil / enchanted / promo foil) + market value_per_unit. `Sealed` rarity rows are booster boxes, not cards — excluded. At build time this is baked into a compact `server/src/ownership.json` the seed action imports (server actions cannot read workspace files).

### LorcanaJSON full card catalog (card universe + art + stats)
**Used by**: seedBinder (catalog rows, slimmed snapshot bundled server-side as fallback-free primary), source of every card's Ravensburger image path
**Test command**: `curl -sL 'https://lorcanajson.org/files/current/en/allCards.json'` (HTTP 200, 9,553,983 bytes)
**Sample output**: 3,556 cards; sets meta covers sets 1-14 (The First Chapter..Hyperia City), 15/16 future sets with 0 cards, quests Q1/Q2/Q3. Each card: fullName, setCode, number, rarity, foilTypes, externalLinks.tcgPlayerId, images.full = `https://api.lorcana.ravensburger.com/images/en/set1/1_ea50....jpg`. Set 1 rarities: 204 base + 12 Enchanted + 29 Special. Set 14: 204 base + 18 Epic + 18 Enchanted + 2 Iconic + 15 Special.
**Processing**: one binder slot per catalog card (set + collector number). Rarity `Special` cards are promos/special printings — their fullIdentifier suffix (`1/P1`, `01/D23`, `1/C1`, `1/CC1`, promo paths like `images/en/promo1/...`) assigns them to promo-binder families (P1, P2, P3, P4, PD1, D23, Challenge, others present) instead of the main set. Main-set cards group by setCode 1-14; quests Q1-Q3 get their own small section. Epics/Enchanteds/Iconics are numbered slots beyond the base count, exactly as his Shiny discriminators show (`207/204` etc.).

### Ravensburger official image CDN (real card art)
**Used by**: cardImage action (fetch once -> ctx.blobs cache -> blob URL), rendered for both owned (full color) and missing (ghosted) cards
**Test command**: `curl -sI 'https://api.lorcana.ravensburger.com/images/en/set13/1_9e5034ef64dbfe30344c5dace27116d15a8cf47f.jpg'`
**Sample output**: HTTP 200, `Content-Type: image/jpeg` — real official card scans as JPEG (not AVIF), for main sets, promos (`/images/en/promo1/`, `/images/en/d23/`, `/images/en/challenge1/`), and quests alike, via each catalog card's images.full URL.
**Processing**: lazy per-card fetch inside the action the first time a pocket is viewed; bytes stored with ctx.blobs (image/jpeg), only the blob key persisted in ctx.db. Client requests images only when a pocket nears the viewport (IntersectionObserver, ~900px margin), so the continuous scroll through a full set never downloads the ~3,500-image universe at once.

### Ownership join verification (Shiny <-> catalog)
**Used by**: seedBinder join logic
**Test command**: background python probe joining the CSV to live Lorcast by tcgplayer_id (same join key LorcanaJSON carries)
**Sample output**: 3,505 / 3,705 CSV rows matched by tcgplayer_id (9,119 / 9,500 cards). Unmatched buckets diagnosed: Illumineer's Quest rows (LorcanaJSON Q1/Q2 carry tcgPlayerId — covered), Epic Foil rows (LorcanaJSON includes Epics as numbered cards with tcgPlayerId — covered), `Sealed` product rows (excluded by design). Remaining stragglers (some promos, Hyperia `None` rows) get a fallback join on (set, collector number from discriminator, normalized name) and any still-unmatched owned printing is surfaced in the seed report counts rather than silently dropped.

### Financial metrics section (added Nov 2, 2026; rebuilt same day to export-exact data)
**Requested by**: `Are there more? Can we add financial metrics to this binder?` + `like create like a section for it?` + per-card tab (`keep track of the card's individual value`) + charts (`I don't want just numerical values. I also want to see a chart.`) + history (`the chart starts from the time that we start this collection`) + daily updates (`I want you to update these financials daily.`)
**First-build defect, fixed**: the original ownership.ts applied ONE per-unit price to all finishes of a printing. In Shiny, Regular and Cold Foil rows share one TCGplayer id but carry different value_per_unit/paid_per_unit (e.g. foil $0.47 vs regular $0.14), so binder totals came out $5,405.86 against the export's true $4,418.05. ownership.ts was regenerated from the Nov 1, 2026 (11:27 PM) CSV with a per-finish breakdown (byFinish: qty + value/paid per unit per finish); quantities were unchanged (9,368 cards / 2,812 printings before and after). Join: TCGplayer id primary; (set, collector number) fallback for Hyperia City (catalog tcg ids absent/stale there); promo-family + number and name fallbacks for promos whose catalog entries carry no TCGplayer id. 0 rows unmatched.
**Tie-out (verified by recomputation from the CSV)**: binder scope 9,368 cards, value $4,100.71, paid $4,553.01; sealed products 14 items / $306.25 / $268.70; Ink Drop tokens + Floodborn puzzle pieces 118 items / $11.09 / $32.99 (counted in the export, not binder cards). Export scope total: 9,500 cards, $4,418.05 value, $4,854.70 paid, net −$436.65, avg $0.47/card — exactly the export's own sums. Foil-family finishes (Cold Foil, Epic Foil, Enchanted, Enchanted Foil, Promo Foil, Promo Cold Foil, Foil) hold $2,588.48. Per-set export aggregates (value/paid verified: Hyperia City $790.95/$1,213.79, Attack of the Vine! $496.58/$615.37, Whispers in the Well $431.23/$277.49, Disney Lorcana Promos $424.07/$495.86) are bundled in server/src/data/exportFinance.ts with the sealed item list.
**Used by**: getFinance (Finances tab: totals, value-over-time line chart, value-by-set and paid-vs-value charts, per-set table, most-valuable items merged cards+sealed, rarity chart, price tiers, spare/beyond-playset values) and getCard's finance block (card sheet Finances tab: per-finish value/paid, totals, gain/loss, paid-vs-value mini bar). Values compute live from stored per-finish prices x current counts; seedBinder backfills price fields (never counts) on load for binders seeded by the first build.
**History**: finance_snapshots table seeded with the five real export snapshots supplied by Anthony — Oct 25, 2026 ($4,419.58 / $4,685.34 / 9,336), Oct 27 ($4,419.98 / $4,731.20 / 9,437), Oct 29 ($4,485.73 / $4,855.63 / 9,446), Nov 1 first ($4,413.98 / $4,850.63 / 9,498), Nov 1 latest ($4,418.05 / $4,854.70 / 9,500). Oct 25 is labeled the start of tracked collection history; no earlier points exist or are invented. New exports (standing auto-update rule) and daily re-pricings append points.
**Daily re-pricing**: cron `lorcana-binder-daily-finance-refresh` (daily 06:30 America/Chicago, space_action) calls refreshFinance, which spawns an agent task (dedupeKey per day, expectsAction saveRepricing). The task lists owned printings via getRepricingTargets, fetches current USD prices (Lorcast API prices: usd / usd_foil), and saves via saveRepricing, which updates per-finish VALUES only (paid untouched) and appends that day's snapshot only when >=50% of owned printings were priced. The Finances tab also has a manual "Re-price now" button calling the same action.
**Per-card price history**: card_price_history table seeded from all five real export files on disk (each file's totals verified against the recorded snapshots before use — Oct 25 file sums to $4,419.58, Oct 27 to $4,419.98, Oct 29 to $4,485.73, Nov 1 first to $4,413.98, Nov 1 latest to $4,418.05) using the same join as ownership.ts: 14,035 points across 2,812 printings, each point the printing's quantity-weighted average value per copy at that export. The card sheet's Finances tab renders it as a classic Wikipedia-style line chart (white plot, single blue line with data points, gridlines, labeled axes — Anthony's reference style). saveRepricing appends/refreshes one daily point per priced card, so each card's chart also grows a point per day.

### Foil treatment + chart style (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Is there a way for you to make the artwork look more foily-ish for the cards that are foils?` and the chart-style reference link (classic Wikipedia line chart, Pushkin population example) + `I want that chart. Tracking individual card values as well and I want that chart in the finance area when you click directly onto a specific card.`
**Foil treatment**: pure presentation — owned foil pockets (the foil pocket of a pair, and single pockets whose finishes are foil-family) get an iridescent rainbow sheen overlay (`.foil-overlay`, screen blend) plus a slight saturation lift on the art; the large card view adds an animated shine sweep (`.foil-shine-band`, disabled under prefers-reduced-motion; pockets stay static for scroll performance). Missing cards never get the sheen — the ghost treatment takes precedence, per Anthony's standing spec.
**Color follow-up (Nov 2, 2026)**: `Make that shimmer ... a little bit more colorful.` (verbatim user request) — the foil sheen is now a fuller, more saturated rainbow (pink → orange → gold → green → cyan → violet → magenta at roughly double the previous opacity), the animated shine sweep carries the same rainbow tint instead of plain white, and foil art saturation is lifted (1.18 → 1.32). Presentation only; ghosted missing cards still never get the sheen.
**Shimmer follow-up (Nov 2, 2026)**: `Could you make the action action of it a little bit faster? Also can you make the shimmer happen on the main page where I'm looking? at the four by Infinity area?` (verbatim user request; "four by Infinity" = the main four-across binder grid). The sweep now also runs on owned foil pockets in the main grid (`.foil-shine-band-grid`, mounted only once a pocket nears the viewport, phase-staggered per pocket so the page doesn't pulse in unison) and the cycle is faster everywhere: 3.8s → 2.2s with the sweep portion of the cycle widened (hold ends at 38%, sweep completes by 72%).
**Chart style**: both value-over-time charts (collection in Finances, per-card in the card sheet's Finances tab) use the classic Wikipedia style he linked: single blue line with data points, dashed gridlines, labeled axes (time on X, USD value on Y with an axis title), and a chart title. **Dark-background follow-up (Nov 2, 2026)**: `On the value over time graph chart. the line chart, can you make the background a dark background instead of white?` (verbatim user request) — both line charts now render on the binder's dark surface (#151a20) with muted gridlines/axis labels (#9aa5b1 on #2a323c), a dark tooltip, and a lighter blue line (#6b9eff) for contrast; the white Wikipedia plot background is superseded. The by-set breakdowns stay simple bar charts on the dark theme. Per-card history is built only from the five named export files in ~/workspace/user/files (a97a79… Oct 25, 1cc3d7… Oct 27, b62ef1… Oct 29, 63392e… Nov 1 first, 6533e1… Nov 1 latest), matched by TCGplayer id with set + collector number + name + variant fallbacks (the same join as ownership), valued at value_per_unit (quantity-weighted across the finishes he owned that day).

### Shiny PRO restyle (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Can you make our app look a little bit more like this?` with a screenshot of the Shiny PRO app he sent (~/workspace/user/files/9034_0_8k04.jpg — used as a visual reference only; no Shiny branding, logo, or mascot copied).
**Applied**: deep blue-black background (#060913) with a subtle CSS starfield/nebula texture (radial-gradient layers on `.app-shell`); bright periwinkle/cornflower blue accent (#6e9bff, pale #a9c3ff) replacing the jade-teal for numbers, links, highlights, bars, and selected chips (the --gold variable names now hold periwinkle values); Finance view opens with a Shiny-style hero — "YOUR COLLECTION" label, very large bold blue total-value number, "TOTAL PAID · $X (−8.94%)" line with green/red percent, and a three-stat row (gain/loss, cards & items, avg per card); both value-over-time charts are now smooth (monotone) glowing blue area charts with a soft blue gradient fill under the line, superseding the earlier Wikipedia-style straight line on dark; filter chips, the Binder/Finances toggle, the All/Owned/Missing filter, and the card-sheet tabs are pill-shaped with the selected pill filled blue; most-valuable rows gained real-art thumbnails, blue secondary labels, and per-item paid gain/loss percentages like the reference's Top Collected list; headings/display numbers switched from serif to a clean bold sans.
**Unchanged**: all data, layout structure, card pockets, ghosted missing cards, and the colorful foil shimmer (`.foil-overlay`, `.foil-img`, `.foil-shine-band` rules untouched). Same build also adds the auto-retry Anthony approved after a one-off "binder couldn't load" failure: getBinder/getFinance/getSection retry 3 times with backoff while staying in the loading state.
**Load-fix details (same build, per inspection b23a6075)**: the three queries also set refetchOnReconnect/refetchOnWindowFocus; the "The binder couldn't load. Try again" banner only renders once retries are exhausted (react-query isError), with the underlying error message in small text under it and the manual Try again refetch kept; seedBinder now fires only when getBinder returns zero groups, not on every load of a populated binder.
**Card-detail Back button (same build)**: Anthony asked to leave a card's detail view with the device/browser Back button instead of only the Close button. Opening a card pushes one history entry; popstate closes the sheet and returns to the binder with section, filter, and scroll untouched (the page behind the fixed overlay never unmounts). UI closes (a new labeled "← Back" pill at the top of the sheet, the kept Close button, backdrop tap, post-save close) route through history.back() when an entry was pushed, so entries are consumed exactly once and never stack. If history is unavailable in the frame, the buttons fall back to closing the sheet directly.
**Back-gesture clarification (same build)**: Anthony clarified the point is his Samsung system back gesture — from the single-card view it used to exit the binder entirely to the files view. The pushState-on-open + popstate-close trap above is exactly the fix: the gesture consumes the detail's history entry and lands back on the card grid he was viewing (scroll position included, since the grid never unmounted); back from the top-level grid is untouched and behaves normally.
**Load improvements (same build, from inspection b23a6075; cold launch ~2.8s, ~9.4s settle, getBinder + getFinance both scanning all 3,556 rows at once)**: (1) seedBinder gated to an actually-empty binder (above). (2) getFinance no longer loads with the binder on first paint — it loads when the Finances view opens, or in the background via requestIdleCallback (2.5s timeout fallback) so the header totals still appear on their own. (3) Sections already loaded on demand (current section only); pocket rendering is now progressive — first 160 pockets paint immediately, more append as a scroll sentinel nears (search jumps expand the full section), and pocket art lazy-loads near the viewport as before. (4) The getBinder response (section summaries + totals, a few KB) is cached in localStorage (`lorcana-binder-response-v1`) and used as react-query initialData, so repeat opens paint instantly and revalidate in the background.
**On-device card art cache (same build)**: Anthony asked for card photos saved locally on his device. `client/src/artCache.ts`: a versioned Cache Storage cache (`lorcana-art-v1`; older `lorcana-art-*` versions pruned at startup) populated as art is viewed. Grid pockets/list thumbnails cache a client-downscaled 320px-wide JPEG; full resolution is cached only when a card's detail view opens (which also backfills that card's grid thumbnail from the same download — one shared in-flight fetch per card). A localStorage index tracks insertion order; caps are 1,600 grid / 120 full entries with oldest-first eviction. Any cache failure degrades to the previous server-URL loading.
**Rename + chart removal + finalization (same build)**: Anthony renamed the Finance/Finances section to **Card Performance** — applied to every UI label (Binder/Card Performance toggle, the header's "full breakdown under Card Performance", the card sheet's Card Performance tab and its "This card's performance" heading, and the error state); internal action names (getFinance etc.) are unchanged, as are the section's data, charts, and the daily re-pricing cron. At his direction ("You can remove paid versus value by set. In the finances area graph. That's not needed.") the Paid vs Value by Set grouped bar chart was removed entirely; the per-set value/paid/net table that shared its card stays, retitled "Value and paid, by set", alongside the value-by-set chart and the value-over-time chart. Final direction: NO shareable version — the binder stays private (the shareable idea was explicitly deferred by Anthony until after this private app is finalized); this build closes with a consistency polish pass (uniform section headings, pill controls, card radii, spacing).
**Most Valuable jump pill (same build)**: at Anthony's request ("Make that a clickable little oval thing on the top, so I can just click right to it"), a blue-outlined pill labeled "Most Valuable" sits at the top of the Card Performance view and smooth-scrolls straight to the existing "Most valuable items you own" ranked list (thumbnails, values, paid/gain-loss — unchanged).
**Oval search bar (same build)**: Anthony sent a second reference photo (~/workspace/user/files/9038_1_tofn.jpg, Shiny's Disney Lorcana set screen) and asked for the search to look exactly like it: a full-width pill bar, ~52px tall, dark charcoal fill (#17171c) with a thin subtle dark-grey border (#2e2e38), a medium-grey magnifier icon inside on the left, and the placeholder "Search your cards…" in medium grey (#8a8a96) italic — never blue or white; typed text stays light. Search behavior (type → jump to and highlight the card) is unchanged.

### Pocket arrangement (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: Anthony displays his physical binders with the regular card and its foil version in adjacent pockets (regular first, foil next to it) and asked the digital binder to match (recorded in MEMORY.md).
**Implementation**: no schema change — getSection/getPage expand each printing into pockets server-side (pocketsOf in actions.ts). Main-set base cards always get a Regular pocket + a foil pocket (Cold Foil), each with its own owned quantity and ghost state; a printing he owns in both finish families splits the same way; foil-only printings (Epic/Enchanted/Iconic rarities) and single-version promos/quests keep one pocket. getBinder totals and section counts are pocket counts. Tapping either pocket opens the same card sheet, whose count editor still works per finish.

### Most Valuable pill rename (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Let's rename that to most valuable cards.` (verbatim user request) — the oval quick-jump pill at the top of the Card Performance view now reads "Most Valuable Cards" instead of "Most Valuable". Its styling and behavior (smooth-scroll to the "Most valuable items you own" list) are unchanged.

### Section picker dropdown (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Make that set one, two, three, four a selectable drop down not span across the whole side there.` (verbatim user request) — the binder's section selector at the top (previously a full-width horizontally scrolling row of pill chips for Set 1, Set 2, Set 3, Set 4, promos, quests…) is now a single compact native `<select>` dropdown that sizes to its content (max 72vw / 320px, truncates long names) with a "Section" label and a compact owned/total count beside it. Options read "Set 1 — The First Chapter" etc. (short label + full section name). Selecting an option calls the same `pickGroup` handler as the chips did — section, filter, scroll-to-top, and search-jump behavior are unchanged; no data or action changes.

### Set-selection tile grid (Nov 2, 2026) — supersedes the dropdown above
**Requested by**: `Better but instead of that selection I want it to look like this photo I'm about to upload to you.` + `Also, I want you to have the progress bar at the bottom like this. displaying.` + `Have all the pertinent data that this site is showing as well.` (verbatim user requests) with the reference screenshot of Shiny's Disney Lorcana set-selection screen saved at ~/workspace/user/files/9040_2_tlb1.jpg (visual reference only; no Shiny branding or set-logo image files copied — each tile's wordmark is the set's name typeset in a per-set accent color echoing its logo colors).
**Applied**: the compact dropdown is replaced by a set-selection screen that is now the binder's landing view. Main sets are grouped under collapsible year headings "Disney Lorcana 2026 / 2025 / 2024 / 2023" (newest first; sets 11–14 = 2026, 7–10 = 2025, 3–6 = 2024, 1–2 = 2023, matching the reference's groupings), each heading a button with a small collapse chevron at the right. Sets render as a 3-across grid of dark rounded tiles; each tile centers the set's wordmark and carries a thin progress bar along its bottom edge inside the tile (bright blue #6e9bff fill with rounded ends over a dark track, width = owned/total). Under each tile: the set name in white and grey "owned / total • percent%" text — the same pocket counts getBinder already reported (e.g. the reference's format, with this binder's own numbers). Promo families and Illumineer's Quests keep their own tiles under "Promos" and "Illumineer's Quests" headings so every section stays reachable. Tapping a tile calls the same `pickGroup` handler the dropdown used (section loads, scroll to top); a "← All sets" pill at the top of a section returns to the grid, and search jumps land directly in the section as before. No data, action, or other view changes.

### Card detail restyle + bottom navigation + set tile logos (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Also when you zoom into a card I want it to look more professional like this photo I'm about to send to you.` (Shiny card detail screenshot, ~/workspace/user/files/9042_3_u0qr.jpg) + `I want a selectable menu at the very bottom of the page as depicted in the photo I'm about to send you. Let's create a home area but we'll figure out what we want to build out on that home. Let's create a searchable area. A social area but let's keep the social blank — we'll have the tab there and you could put in there "in progress" ... Add the portfolio tab as well.` (bottom-bar reference: ~/workspace/user/files/9044_4_f3it.jpg, Home highlighted) + set tile logos from the set-screen photo (~/workspace/user/files/9040_2_tlb1.jpg).
**Card detail**: the card sheet is now a full-screen dark view: a blurred, darkened blow-up of the card's own artwork fills the background (fixed layer, gradient into #05070d), a back arrow sits in a dark rounded square at top-left (the Samsung back-gesture history trap and the Close button are unchanged), and the card renders large and centered (w-72/sm:w-80) with a deep soft shadow. Below it, a Shiny-style title block: card name + printed collector number in bold white on the left; on the right the market price per copy in large white with its change underneath in green/red — computed from the card's own tracked price history (latest point vs the previous point, labeled "since <prev label>"), never invented; when a card has fewer than two history points no change line shows. Next line: set name in blue, then "• rarity" in white. All existing per-card content (stats, rules text, owned regular/cold foil plus/minus editor with Save, and the Card Performance tab with per-finish value/paid, gain/loss, paid-vs-value bar, and the value-over-time chart) is unchanged below the title block. No data changes.
**Bottom navigation**: a fixed bottom tab bar (Home / Search / Binder / Social / Portfolio, line icons, active tab in blue with a pill highlight behind the icon, safe-area padded; main content gained bottom padding so nothing hides behind it). Home is the new default view: a clean placeholder ("we'll build it out once you decide") with a collection-at-a-glance card and shortcut rows to Binder, Search, and Portfolio. Search switches to the binder view and focuses the existing oval search bar. Binder is the previous binder view, unchanged. Social renders only the words "In progress" (summary header and footer hidden there too) — blank until Anthony decides what goes there. Portfolio is the Card Performance section, unchanged; the old Binder/Card Performance pill toggle at the top was removed since the tab bar replaces it, and the header summary now points to "the Portfolio tab". The daily re-pricing cron and all action names are untouched.
**Refinements (same day, follow-up messages)**: the bar matches his photo (9044_4_f3it.jpg) exactly — a floating, inset, fully rounded dark bar whose selected tab is a filled dark-teal pill wrapping icon AND label together. The Social page now shows a "Social" heading with "In progress" in bright amber (#ffb020) beneath it (his words: "make it say social, but then in different colors say in progress"), otherwise still blank. The Most Valuable Cards list was removed from the main Card Performance page (he found the page too long); the "Most Valuable Cards" pill now opens its own page with the same ranked list (same data, rows still open the card sheet) and normal back behavior — opening pushes one history entry so the Samsung/browser Back and the on-page "← Back" button both return to Card Performance. The app icon was regenerated at his request to an open book (the icon pool's open-book asset); the pool offers no variant with card artwork printed on the pages, so the pages are blank — disclosed in the build report.
**Set tile logos**: main-set tiles now show the set's real logo artwork centered (`client/src/assets/set-logos/set-<n>.png`, imported by the client): sets 4–14 were carefully cropped from Anthony's supplied Shiny set-screen screenshot (9040_2_tlb1.jpg) with the screenshot's dark tile background keyed to transparency; set 1 (The First Chapter) came from card-binder.com's collection logo file, set 2 (Rise of the Floodborn) from the official Ravensburger logo lockup PNG via vertigo6.nl (cropped to the set-name block, dropping the Lorcana/Ravensburger lockup elements), and set 3 (Into the Inklands) from lorcanacollectors.com's standalone logo (white background unmultiplied to transparency). Year groupings, the blue progress bar along each tile's bottom edge, and the name + "owned / total • percent%" captions are exactly as before.
**Promo tile artwork (follow-up, same day)**: Anthony asked whether the promo sets have artwork too, noting D23 must have some. D23 does: the tile for the D23 group now shows the 2024 D23 Collection box-front artwork (navy box, gold Lorcana emblem and character outlines, gold "D23 Collection" lettering), downloaded from Screen Rant's image CDN (static0.srcdn.com, the 2024/07 D23 Collection box photo), cropped to the box front, stored as `client/src/assets/set-logos/promo-d23.png`. **Requested by**: three follow-ups from Anthony — (1) the D23 Collection tile's picture looks a little too big, size it down so it sits better in its tile; for the other promo tiles (P1, P2, P3, P4, PD1, Challenge…) make the photo bigger and put the label to the right of the photo. A shared `effectiveSealed` server helper applies overrides (value/paid scale with quantity) and now feeds getSealed, getFinance (totals, per-set chart, most-valuable list), and saveRepricing's daily snapshot, so the whole app stays consistent when quantities change. New `setSealedQty` action validates the item name against the export list and upserts the override. Each Sealed tile now shows "Owned ×N" with − / + round buttons (minus disabled at 0), wired through a mutation that refreshes the sealed and finance queries; name, set, value, and paid lines are unchanged.

## Agent-task sources
None.

## Long-term data behavior
- **Refresh policy**: none automatic. Ownership reflects his Nov 1, 2026 Shiny export (the newest he has provided); the two TCGplayer orders that arrived after it are not in that export. The card detail view lets him fix any slot by hand (mark owned, set quantity/finishes), stored as durable DB overrides the seed never overwrites. No cron is installed (none requested).
- **Growth**: fixed catalog of ~3,556 slots (sets 1-14, promo families, quests Q1-Q3). Image blobs grow lazily as he scrolls; each card fetched at most once.
- **Ordering**: sets in release order 1-14, then promo families, then quests; within a set strictly by collector number; rendered as one continuous grid, 4 across all the way down (getSection returns the whole filtered section; the old getPage action remains for compatibility but the UI no longer pages).
- **Time semantics**: no datetimes drive behavior; the only date shown is the export date label (Nov 1, 2026) taken from his file's provenance in memory.

Every book phrasing resolves to the same single pool asset (`entertainment-media:book-open-front-view`) — an open book with blank cream pages; other phrasings resolve to unrelated objects (printer, gift card, folder). The clean open-book icon was restored as the final state rather than shipping a worse unrelated icon.

## Rejected approaches
- **Tried**: Lorcast API (`https://api.lorcast.com/v0/cards/search?q=set:1`) as the catalog
  **Why rejected**: set search caps at the base set (204 results, max collector number 204) even though Epics/Enchanteds exist in its data, and its only images are AVIF on cards.lorcast.io. LorcanaJSON covers the full numbered universe with official JPEG art in one file.
- **Tried**: lorcana-api.com `/api/cards/all`
  **Why rejected**: returns the docs site's HTML, not JSON.
- **Tried**: local snapshots `~/workspace/lorcana/lorcast_all.json` / `lorcana_all.json` / `pool_full.json`
  **Why rejected**: stale/partial — lorcast_all has no promos or beyond-204 numbers, lorcana_all lacks set 14 and promos, pool_full covers only sets 9-14 (deck-building pool, 1,138 printings). The binder needs all 14 sets + promos + quests.
- **Tried**: reusing the earlier shareable static binder's designed card faces
  **Why rejected**: Anthony explicitly traded shareability for real artwork: 'load the images in for me for my private.'

**Requested by**: `On the opening page on home it says your collection at a glance can you I plan on adding in more additional cards besides Lorcana. I plan on adding in Pokemon and also what's the other one called? Magic the Gathering. Can you have a picture of Lorcana that sits in between the 3685 of 6433 pockets filled have just underneath that the icon of Lorcana and any other collections that you have and then below that have collection value at $4400.` (verbatim user request)
**Layout (top to bottom, exactly the order he listed)**: "Your collection at a glance" heading → Lorcana picture → live pockets-filled count ("3,685 of 6,433 pockets filled · 57%" — live from getBinder totals, not hard-coded) with a progress bar → "Your collections" icon row → collection value below (live from getFinance, ~$4,418 at his Nov 1 export; he quoted ~$4,400).
**Collections row**: three tiles. Lorcana is the live collection — its icon is a circular crop of the banner artwork, labeled with its live pocket count, and tapping it opens the binder. Pokémon and Magic: The Gathering are collections he PLANS to add (he owns none in this app yet), so their tiles use simple drawn icons (a red/white ball for Pokémon, an orange flame spark for Magic), are dashed/dimmed, and are labeled "Planned" — no counts or values are invented for them.
**Lorcana picture provenance**: `client/src/assets/lorcana-glance-banner.webp` — a generated painterly illustration made for this slot at build time (golden inkwell spilling blue/violet ink, floating blank cards, storybook castle, starry deep-navy background matching the app theme). It is decoration in an illustrated style, not official Lorcana artwork and not a photo of a real product; it carries no text or logos.

Three across and then after a third one then start another row three across from there and so on.` (verbatim user request) — the row is a centered flex-wrap: each tile is one third of the row width, tiles flow three-across, and any partial next row centers itself instead of hugging the left, so future collections slot in on either side and stay centered.

### Follow-ups folded into the sealed-photo build (Nov 2, 2026)
**Home shortcut copy**: the Home "Sealed" shortcut subtitle said "Sealed boxes and troves on display in acrylic cases" — Anthony owns no trove; corrected to "Sealed boxes and packs on display in acrylic cases."
**Single pack per tile**: Anthony repeated the point with the Azurite fan as the example ("you have three booster packs. I would only have the image of one booster pack") — the four replacements above satisfy it; every sealed tile now shows exactly one product.
**Hyperia box size**: the first sealed tile (Hyperia City Booster Box) spanned two columns at phone width (`col-span-2 sm:col-span-1`), making it look oversized next to the packs; the span is removed so all fourteen tiles render at the same size.
**Search Back-button fix**: `When you're under the search tab and you do the back button we're still experiencing the same issue we were before the back button is taking us out of the app completely. If anything it should bringing us back to the homepage.` (verbatim user request) — the floating search overlay now uses the same history trap as the card sheet and finance sub-pages: opening pushes one history entry (`{lorcanaSearch: true}`), so device/browser Back closes the overlay and lands on the view underneath (Home by default) instead of exiting the app. UI closes (backdrop, ✕, Escape) route through history.back() so the entry is consumed exactly once, and picking a result hands the entry to the card sheet (flag transfer + openCard's replaceState), so Back from that card also lands on the underlying view.

**Definitive acrylic reference (same day)**: Anthony sent a second photo (~/workspace/user/files/9060_7_27ij.jpg) — a single Pokémon Fossil pack upright in a clear slab-style acrylic case on a small black stand, dark background — as what he means by "a pack in an acrylic case", asking all sealed images to look like that. **Most Valuable thumbnails (same day)**: sealed rows in Portfolio → Most Valuable Cards showed a black tile with only the word "Sealed"; TopItemThumb now renders the item's own Sealed-shelf product photo (SEALED_PHOTOS by exact name) in that spot, falling back to the label tile only if a photo is ever missing.

**Most Valuable sealed rows tappable (same day)**: Anthony also noted the sealed entries in Most Valuable Cards weren't selectable like the other items. They are now buttons: tapping one closes the sub-page (consuming its history entry) and switches to the Sealed shelf, which scrolls that item's tile into view with a blue highlight ring; arriving via the Sealed tab or Home shortcut clears the focus so no stale highlight shows.

### Approved competitor-research picks: scanning, price-to-complete, price alerts, Best Offer (Nov 2, 2026)
**Approved by Anthony** (from the Collection App Feature Ideas board; verbatim approvals in MEMORY.md): camera scanning ("throw camera scanning in there automatically, that's a must have for sure"), price-to-complete + one-click TCGplayer buying ("that would be amazing... like we've been doing"), price alerts (his design: alert setter on the item detail, triggered alerts shown on Home in translucent red; v1 example requested), and a Best Offer element under the card in card detail linking straight to that printing on TCGplayer. Trading (pick #2) is discussion-only — NOT built.
**Price-to-complete** (with his CORRECTION applied): covers the COMPLETE set — every printing and finish including Enchanteds and Iconics; his personal master-set rule is NOT baked into the feature. Per-section missing/cost figures come from the cards table's pockets; price per missing pocket = DB per-finish price when tracked, else the Lorcast snapshot in `server/src/data/catalogPrices.ts` (2,867 printings by TCGplayer id, pulled Oct 26, 2026 from ~/workspace/lorcana/lorcast_all.json — labelled wherever shown). Unpriced pockets are counted and disclosed, never guessed. getBinder group rows now carry missingPockets/costToComplete/unpricedMissing (shown on set tiles and the section header). `getSetCompletion` returns the full missing list sorted by price with TCGplayer ids; links use https://www.tcgplayer.com/product/<id> (canonical product URL; the id is the card's verified TCGplayer ID from his export/Lorcast — tcgplayer.com itself bot-blocks text fetching, so the page could not be fetch-verified). `requestBuyList` spawns Jarvis's agent task with the full list and Anthony's standing cart rules (Near Mint, ≤$3 single-card shipping, no checkout — his approval stays final); the UI labels it as a handoff, not an in-app purchase. Daily repricing now prices the WHOLE catalog (getRepricingTargets returns all rows; saveRepricing stores market prices for unowned cards with paid 0 and no history points), so completion figures update daily.
**Camera scanning**: ScanPanel (entries in the Binders view and the Search overlay) — live getUserMedia viewfinder with Capture when the device allows it, plus a photo input (`capture="environment"`) that always works. Photos are downscaled to 1280px JPEG on-device and identified server-side by `identifyCard` via ctx.inference vision (name, set, collector number, foil guess; binderPage flag for multi-card pages), then matched against the catalog by name/number/set scoring. Results show art, printing, market value, owned qty/finishes, a Regular/Foil toggle (foil from a photo is a best guess — disclosed in the UI), and Add buttons using the same updateOwnership as card detail; a session list accumulates adds for continuous stack scanning. History handling uses the same pushState trap family as the other sheets, with an entry transfer from the search overlay.

**Scan reliability pass (Nov 3, 2026, v1.48.0 — Anthony's top priority after his first official in-app scan hung on "Identifying…" with no visible snapshot, then returned the wrong card):** Diagnosis: the scanner did draw a JPEG snapshot but never showed it (only an "Identifying…" overlay on the still-live video), and neither client nor server had any timeout on the single vision call, so it could pause indefinitely; the wrong match came from a loose matcher (a partial-name substring score of 45 exactly met the acceptance threshold with no number read; with a number read, pool-internal substring matches could still win and set agreement was only a +15 bonus, with no confidence gate). Fix: (1) Client — Capture freezes the snapshot on screen (shutter flash + hapticConfirm), a card-outline guide sits in the viewfinder, Identifying shows an elapsed timer + stage text + Cancel, a 45s client ceiling stops the wait keeping the photo with Try again / Retake, and a generation counter makes late results after cancel/timeout no-ops; picked photos show their preview immediately; scanning another re-attaches the live stream to the remounted video element (it previously could go black). (2) Server identifyCard — the vision read also returns ink color and ink cost; matching is corroboration-gated: a read collector number stays a hard filter, a definite match requires an exact full/base title read with no tie in the number pool (or a partial read corroborated by set + ink/cost), an exact title naming several printings needs set/ink/cost to single one out, and everything weaker returns `ambiguous` candidate printings (up to 4) for the user to pick from instead of a definite wrong card; definite matches carry readName/readNumber and the UI shows a "Read from your photo as…" note when the read differs. A 75s server-side Promise.race ceiling fails the action with a plain retryable message. The wrong-card catalog search on the result screen is unchanged and now also sits under the ambiguous picker ("None of these? Use the search below.").
**Price alerts v1**: `price_alerts` table (migration 0007); setPriceAlert/getPriceAlerts/deletePriceAlert actions; triggered state computed live from current unit values (cards: marketValue; sealed: export unit value). PriceAlertBox sits in the card sheet's Card Performance tab and in the sealed sheet; Home renders triggered alerts in translucent red at the very top.
**Best Offer**: card detail shows a "Best Offer · TCGplayer" link under the title block when the printing has a TCGplayer id, with the tracked market price; getCard now returns tcgplayerId.

### Collector ID — oval QR identifier at the top of Home (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Now Jarvis let's create a unique identifier we can use a QR code but in an oval shape up at the top have a username that the user picks out along with a photo of their choice.` (verbatim user request)
**Applied**: an oval (full stadium/rounded-full) Collector ID card sits at the very top of Home: his chosen photo in a circle on the left (falling back to his username's initial), his username (@ARUIKIS) and collector code in the middle, and a QR code on the right. Under Anthony's earlier direction the triggered price-alert banners sat first on Home; this newer request puts the identifier at the top, so the ID card renders above the alert banners and everything else on Home.
**Identifier**: new `collector_profile` table (migration 0008, single row id=1) holding username, collector code, and photo blob key. The code (format `MC-XXXX-XXXX`, unambiguous alphabet, crypto-random) is generated once by `getCollectorId` on first read and never changes, so the QR stays a stable unique identifier. The starting username is ARUIKIS — Anthony's real Shiny handle and cross-platform gamer tag (from MEMORY.md) — and it is fully editable in the card's Edit panel (3–20 letters/numbers/underscores, validated server-side by `setCollectorUsername`); the QR re-encodes on every username change.
**Photo**: the Edit panel's photo picker takes any image from his device; the client downsizes it to a ≤1280px JPEG (the same conversion the card scanner uses) and `setCollectorPhoto` stores the bytes as an owned blob (`collector-photo/profile.jpg`, only the key persisted). `removeCollectorPhoto` clears it back to the initial-letter avatar. No stock or generated photo is pre-filled — the slot shows his initial until he picks one.
**QR code**: encoded in-app by `client/src/qr.ts`, a dependency-free QR generator written for this feature (byte mode, ECC level L, versions 1–10, Reed–Solomon over GF(256), mask penalty evaluation), rendering as SVG. Payload is plain text a generic scanner can read: `My Collectibles` / `Collector: @<username>` / `ID: <collector code>`. Verified during the build against the reference Python `qrcode` library: data+ECC codewords byte-identical at versions 3 and 9, and OpenCV's QRCodeDetector successfully decoded the generated codes for payloads of 7–100 chars (the real payload is ~55 chars, version 3). No external QR service is called and no remote image is rendered.
**Purpose/provenance**: this is the identifier hook Anthony's parked social/trading design anticipated (MEMORY.md: a shareable app needs "an abuse-resistant access design (e.g. a QR identifier)"). It does not connect any live social or trading system — Friends/Trades remain front-end structure — it gives him the scannable ID those features will use.

### Same-build follow-ups (Nov 2, 2026, evening)
**Sealed artwork — actually rendering (Anthony: "still not seeing artwork in the Sealed section")**: investigation found the 14 product photos, the name→photo map (14/14 exact matches), and the staged build all correct — the audit browser rendered every tile. The likely on-device failure is payload weight + lazy loading in the phone webview: the 14 transparent PNGs totaled 7,678,273 bytes and loaded `loading="lazy"`. Fix shipped: all 14 photos (plus the `sealed-booster-front` fallback) re-encoded as WebP with alpha at ≤480px wide, quality 88 — 908,654 bytes total (~8.5× lighter) — imports switched to `.webp` (PNGs deleted), tile images now load eagerly (no `loading="lazy"`), and in-case padding tightened (px-1.5/pt-1.5/pb-5) so products sit larger. Verified tile by tile in the probe: all 14 images load (0 broken), with screenshots of the top row (Hyperia box, Prerelease, First Chapter, Ursula/Elsa), middle rows (Jafar, Shimmering, Hyperia pack, Floodborn, Inklands, Azurite), and detail sheets for Inklands and Azurite, all showing the real product art.
**Sealed pull-to-refresh (Anthony's bug report)**: refreshing (reload) on the Sealed page bounced him to Home because the view lived only in React state. The main view is now persisted in sessionStorage (`mycollectibles-view-v1`) and restored on load — a pull-to-refresh reload lands back on the same section (verified in the probe: reload on Sealed stayed on Sealed with all 14 tiles); a full app close still starts on Home, since sessionStorage clears with the session.
**Portfolio wording — collection-wide (Anthony's direction)**: Portfolio no longer speaks as if everything is a card. Changed: pill tabs "Where Your Cards Sit by Price" → "Where Your Items Sit by Price" and "Most Valuable Cards" → "Most Valuable Items"; the tiers page title "Where your cards sit by price" → "Where your items sit by price" and its row caption "per-card market price" → "per-item market price"; hero stats "Cards & items" → "Items" and "Avg per card" → "Avg per item" (the top page title was already "Most valuable items you own"). Card-specific wording stays only where it is genuinely about Lorcana cards (binder pocket counts, per-finish and spare-copy analytics, card detail views).
**Collector ID refinement (Anthony's follow-up direction)**: the oval ID is now a LOT smaller — a compact oval chip in the top-right corner of Home (36px photo, @username and code still readable, 36px QR thumbnail). Tapping it opens the full ID view: big photo, username, code, a 256px scannable QR, and the username/photo editor (moved inside). Back/Close use the same one-history-entry trap as the other sheets. The full-width oval from the first build is superseded.
**Trust Score design (Anthony's direction)**: a karma-style score that is verified-only by design. The expanded Collector ID view carries a Trust Score card: score 0 with a breakdown of Confirmed trades (0), Confirmed connections (0), and Feedback from confirmed trades (none yet), and the rules in plain words — a trade counts only when both collectors confirm it (e.g. by scanning each other's Collector ID), feedback counts only after a confirmed trade, connections count only people who scanned the ID and actually connected (not views/taps), no upvotes or self-voting. Social > Friends carries a matching summary card. Front-end structure only, consistent with Social being front-end only: no live events exist yet, so every figure is an honest zero — no score, trade, connection, or feedback was invented.
**Trust Score placement refinement (Anthony, later Nov 2)**: the Trust Score and a "Confirmed connections" count now sit directly under the photo/username/code at the top-center of the expanded Collector ID profile (two profile stats with a divider, captioned "Verified events only — connections count people who scanned your ID and connected, not views or taps"); the card below the QR is now titled "Trust Score breakdown" and keeps the per-event rows and rules. The picture and username are centered at the top of the profile view (Anthony corrected an upper-left placement to top-middle).
**Item detail charting + Best Offer on EVERY item (Anthony's rule, Nov 2, 2026)**: a Hyperia City card detail showed no chart and no where-to-buy. Root cause found in the data: the lorcana-api catalog snapshot carries NO TCGplayer ID for any of Hyperia City's 242 printings (plus Q3 Hunny Rescue 38/38, Promo Set 4 8/8, and scattered others — 327 of 3,556 cards total), and both Best Offer links render only when an ID exists; the value-over-time chart likewise vanished entirely for cards with no tracked price points (and the empty note only showed for owned cards). Fixes: (1) TCGplayer IDs recovered from Anthony's five Shiny export CSVs — matched on set name + printed number + card name, zero conflicting keys, regular and foil share one product ID in Shiny's data — into `server/src/data/tcgplayerBackfill.ts` (196 cards, incl. 196 of Hyperia's 242); `tcgIdOf()` applies it in getCard, completion lists, pocket pricing, and getRepricingTargets, and seedBinder persists it into `cards.tcgplayer_id` where NULL. With IDs in the repricing target list, the daily re-pricing now prices Hyperia cards and appends their chart points (saveRepricing appends a point for every priced card, owned or not). (2) The remaining 131 never-exported printings (Hyperia Enchanteds/Iconics, unowned promos/quests) still show the Best Offer element via `BestOfferFallback`: it links to tcgplayer.com itself with the card name to search by — a deep search URL could not be verified from this environment (TCGplayer's fetch failed), so no product/search URL was guessed. (3) The chart section now renders on every card and sealed detail: the chart when points exist, otherwise the section shell with an honest "No tracked prices yet" note, owned or not. (4) Graded comics' Performance tab gained both elements: "Best Offer — eBay" (comics trade on eBay; his values are eBay-sold-based; links to ebay.com with the slab title + CGC cert to search by — deep link likewise unverified, none guessed) and a Value over time chart holding the one real valuation point (Oct 29, 2026 checked value), captioned as a single tracked point that grows on re-checks — no invented history. The Paid vs Value BAR chart stays removed per Anthony's earlier direction; only the value-over-time line chart is universal. Lego has no item catalog/details yet, so there is nothing to attach there; future item types follow the same detail pattern (totals block → Best Offer → value-over-time chart).
**Search wording (Anthony, Nov 2, 2026)**: the Search overlay now says "Search your collection" — the input placeholder ("Search your collection…"), the dialog's aria-label, and the input's label ("Search your collection by name") all use collection wording instead of "cards", consistent with the Portfolio rewording.

### Comics section layout cleanup — CLZ-style (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `I think we're going to have to clean up how the comic book section looks` + `Take a look at other app layouts and try to copy off of them. Implement it and then I'll make changes off of that.` (verbatim user requests) — research established comic-collection app layouts, adapt the Comics section to the best of those patterns, implement, and Anthony directs his own tweaks off the result.
**Research**: CLZ Comics (Collectorz.com) patterns, from web search of the app's own descriptions (the collectorz.com page itself could not be screenshot-captured, so the layout copies the documented patterns, not pixels): (1) "Card View" — big cover images 3 per row plus the main comic information (Series, Issue Number, Release Date, Key icon); (2) browse the collection as a list or a "cover wall"; (3) group into folders by series, publisher, grade; (4) key comics carry a key icon and key info (first appearances etc.); (5) a Statistics screen with total comics/series/value and breakdowns; (6) sort by series/issue, value, date. No CLZ branding, artwork, or text copied — layout patterns only.
**Applied (client-only, `client/src/comics.tsx`; all data unchanged — the same 8 real CGC 9.8 slabs, values, cert numbers, and the Slabs / Cover Wall / Key Issues / Signed / Wish List areas)**: (1) The old summary header (an always-full 100% progress bar) became a CLZ Statistics-style strip: three stat blocks — 8 Slabs (all CGC 9.8 · WHITE Pages), ~$1,970 Total value (checked Oct 29, 2026), 3 Key issues (2 of them signed, computed from the data). (2) The Slabs area now opens cover-forward on an "All Slabs" Card View grid (3 across on phone, 4 on wider screens) instead of the group-tile picker; each cover carries the CGC 9.8 grade badge in label colors and, for the three key issues, an amber KEY pill with a key icon (CLZ's key marker); under each cover: title, publisher · date, value (the long variant text moved off the grid — it lives on the detail page). A sort pill row (Value / Title / Publisher) sorts the grid. (3) Grouping moved below the grid as CLZ-style folder lists — "Browse by publisher" (All Comics, Marvel, DC, Dark Horse) and "Browse by label" (CGC Signature Series with a gold dot, CGC Universal with a purple-blue dot) — full-width rows with the group's top cover as thumbnail, slab count and total value, and a chevron; tapping a row opens the same group shelf as before (back/history behavior untouched). The old 3-across group tiles with tiny cover strips and meaningless full progress bars were removed. (4) Group shelves and the Signed area use the same cover-forward grid (no more bordered panel boxes); the Cover Wall tightened to 3 across on phone (4 on wider). (5) Most Valuable Comics ranked list, Key Issues, Wish List, the footer, and the comic detail sheet (cover/label photo toggle, Performance tab, Best Offer, single-point chart) are unchanged. Version bumped 1.1.0 → 1.2.0 (new feature/layout, per Anthony's semver rule).

### Card hover turntable + Home cleanup (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `So Jarvis, this is kind of like how I want all the cards to display... do you see how the card is hovering? It's very clean and it's turning around. Are you able to recreate that or is that too much?` + `Is that going to take up a lot of cache?` (reference photo ~/workspace/user/files/fbdfdeea-b3cb-43ec-ba2b-425fa7309738_9_lw19.jpg — a PSA-graded Pokémon card in its slab floating over a clean white oval base on courtyard.io, slowly turning) + `let's remove the binders tab at the very bottom on the main screen. You can remove the eight graded comics and just put comics there.` Added into the same build (recorded in MEMORY.md): every "Coming soon" label on Home's Your Collections goes yellow, Lego also gets "Coming soon" (yellow), and the "Sets · Sealed · Figures" line comes off the Lego tile.
**Card turntable (client-only; `HoverCard` in App.tsx + `.card-stage/.card-float/.card-turn/.card-pedestal/.card-glare` in theme.css)**: the CardSheet hero is now a turntable stage — the existing full-res art floats (6.5s bob) above a lit oval pedestal with its shadow pooled on it (breathing in sync), slowly swaying ±17° rotateY with a 5° tilt (9s cycle) so the face always stays readable and never mirrors — a full 360° spin was deliberately not used because only the card front exists; a soft diagonal glare (`.card-glare`) drifts across in step with the sway. Foil copies keep `.foil-overlay` + `.foil-shine-band` on top; missing cards hover ghosted as before. Dragging sideways (pointer capture; `touch-action: pan-y` keeps vertical scrolling) or the Left/Right arrow keys (stage is focusable, described by aria-label) turns the card by hand (clamped ±52°); releasing/blur hands it back to the sway. All animation is transform/background-position only on the single hero card — no video, no new images, nothing added to the art cache (answers his cache question: the effect costs no extra storage). `prefers-reduced-motion` disables bob, sway, pedestal, and glare. A small "Drag to turn" caption sits under the pedestal.

All other views keep their bars exactly as before (Binders/Sealed inside card collections; Comics/Lego tab inside those sections).
**Home tiles**: the Comics tile is labeled just "Comics" (the "8 graded comics" subtitle span and the count in its aria-label removed; tile markup now matches Lorcana's single-label style). The Lego tile's "Sets · Sealed · Figures" line is replaced with "Coming soon", and every "Coming soon" subtitle (Pokémon, Magic: The Gathering, Lego, Star Wars Unlimited) is now yellow (#ffb020, semibold) instead of dim grey. No data, actions, or totals changed. Version bumped 1.2.0 → 1.3.0 (new feature, per Anthony's semver rule).

### Central registry wiring — Supabase (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: `Alright, so my question is, the user, the test user you created Jarvis in the social area, is his name now living on that website that we were just that we just created and set up? I just want to make sure that that user ported over the way it should have when you created it to test the site out as well because you said that all the usernames basically live on that site, correct? Let's go ahead and do that.` + `At the very least I want my username on there just to make sure it all works.` (verbatim user requests)
**Live verification before wiring (Nov 2, 2026, ~7:02 PM CST)**: the Shadow Vault Supabase project (Project URL https://iilsbousebrgkmsimucq.supabase.co ; dashboard https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/iilsbousebrgkmsimucq) was probed with its publishable key (the public client key collected Nov 2 for exactly this wiring, saved at ~/workspace/goals/lorcana-collection-binder/hidden_files/supabase-connection.md; the secret key was never read or used). The `profiles` table already existed and held the Jarvis test friend — collector_id "MC-JARVIS", username "Jarvis", trust_score 5, created_at 2026-11-03T01:02:01.957875+00:00 — and the earlier dummy test friend ("MC-TEST-DUMMY", "@dummy.test.friend", trust 12, same timestamp); `collection_items` held the dummy's 3 cards; `connections` and `trust_events` exist and are empty. A publishable-key upsert keyed on the unique collector_id was tested (probe row inserted, read back, deleted) — so YES, Jarvis ported over exactly as he should have, and the app wiring below registers Anthony the same way.
**Server (actions.ts)**: `syncCollectorRegistry` upserts Anthony's own Collector ID (username + his stable collector code from collector_profile) into `profiles`, re-upserts/verifies MC-JARVIS alongside it, reads both rows back (a profile only counts as registered when the read-back shows it), records the verification instant in the new `registry_synced_at` column on collector_profile (migration 0009), and returns the live member list. `getRegistryMembers` reads the live table and flags his row + Jarvis's; `lookupCollector` resolves a typed code or username against the live table (case-insensitive, flags self-matches). Verified in the sandbox against the REAL project: sync returned registered=true, jarvisRegistered=true, and Anthony's row — @ARUIKIS, MC-XFU8-Z5WD, trust 0 — created 2026-11-03T01:08:30Z, confirmed by an independent direct read of the table; lookupCollector("MC-JARVIS") found Jarvis, lookupCollector("ARUIKIS") found Anthony as self. The post-submit action re-runs the same sync once against the production database (idempotent upsert).
**Client**: the Collector ID full view gains a "Shadow Vault server registry" card (live status: on the server / not yet / unreachable, Jarvis's ported-over confirmation, last-verified time, Register / Re-check button) and auto-registers on open whenever his row is missing or his username changed. Social > Friends gains an "On the server" card listing the live registry members (his row flagged "You", Jarvis's flagged "On server"), a "✓ Live on the Shadow Vault server" line on Jarvis's list entry and profile, and Add a Friend by code now checks the live registry (found Jarvis / found member / that's-you / not-registered / server-unreachable states) instead of only the built-in constant. Stale "lives inside this app" copy on Jarvis's profile was corrected. NOT yet live: confirmed connections and trades (connections/trust_events stay empty until that layer is built); the registry's Row Level Security is currently permissive for the prototype — hardening to owner-only policies remains a listed next step before real distribution. Version bumped 1.3.0 → 1.4.0.

### Card chart visibility fix + TCGplayer logo in Best Offer (Nov 2, 2026)
**Requested by**: Anthony re-reported (7:04 PM) that Furfrou - VIP Guest, #1 in Set 14 (Hyperia City) — actually printed "Fru Fru - VIP Guest", uid 14:3266, card id 3270 — STILL showed no price-history graph on its card detail, with spot-checks of other Set 14 details for chart + Best Offer, and directed that the Best Offer element carry the actual TCGplayer LOGO inside it on every item detail.
**Diagnosis**: getCard already returned Fru Fru's five real export points (Oct 25 $0.05 → Nov 1 latest $0.04; verified via the built actions) and its TCGplayer id 722351, but the chart rendered ONLY inside the Card Performance tab — opening a card showed no graph — and card_price_history is seeded exactly once, so a binder seeded by an earlier build could be missing bundled points forever with no backfill.
**Applied**: (1) The chart is factored into `CardValueChart` and now renders on BOTH the main Card tab (visible the moment a card opens) and the Card Performance tab (under Best Offer, as before); the honest "No tracked prices yet" empty state shows in both places when a printing has no points (spot-check: Nick Wilde - Providing Backup #205/204, unowned Epic, returns priceHistory [] and the empty state — nothing fabricated). (2) getCard falls back to the bundled real export history (data/cardHistory.ts) when the database table has no rows for that printing; a non-empty table (which carries the daily re-pricing points) always wins. (3) TCGplayer logo: TCGplayer's own current logo art (card-fan mark + TCGPLAYER wordmark, white on black) from their official CDN — https://tcgplayer-cdn.tcgplayer.com/infinite/images/tcg-content-opengraph.png (found via the image-search skill, preflighted, cropped tight, stored as client/src/assets/tcgplayer-logo.png) — now renders inside every TCGplayer Best Offer element: the card detail title-block link, BestOfferLink (cards + sealed Performance sections), and BestOfferFallback. The comics Best Offer links to eBay (not TCGplayer) and keeps its honest eBay label without the TCGplayer logo; the Japanese-market (SNKRDUNK) Best Offer keeps its source label.

### Cards They Have That I Need + Binders tab removed everywhere (Nov 2, 2026, same build)
**Requested by**: `Create a button, an oval button like we've been creating before, specifically for cards that they have that I need. Cards I don't have, they have. Cards they have that I need. Title it something like that.` + extension: `And have the ability to filter down by — well actually have a search bar there like we have in our search bar. And also have it have a breakdown button... you know how we have a tab you can select what chapter — make it searchable.` + error report: `Also under My Collectibles we have another error. At the bottom I'm still seeing binders at the bottom menu.`
**Cards They Have That I Need (client-only, JarvisFriendProfile)**: an oval pill in the Portfolio pill style (accent border, surface fill, gold-soft text) under the friend's collection, titled "Cards They Have That I Need · N" where N = the friend's cards whose overlap line is "Don't own" (yourQty === 0 from the existing searchCards resolution — no new data). Tapping expands a filtered view of exactly those cards (real artwork via JarvisFriendCardArt, name / set · #printed · finish · qty, red "Don't own" line), each tappable into the normal card detail via the profile's existing onOpenCard. The view carries a search bar styled exactly like the main Search overlay field (52px dark pill #17171c, #2e2e38 border, grey magnifier, grey italic placeholder) filtering by name or name + collector number (numeric tokens match the printed number, same rule as main search), and a "Filter by set" native select (All Sets default; options are the sets actually present in the needed list). Honest empty states: friend has nothing Anthony is missing (pill still shows · 0 and the view explains he owns everything in that collection), and no matches under the current search/filter. The logic is driven by the profile's card list, so every friend profile built on this component (Jarvis today; Dummy Friend was replaced by Jarvis; future friends) gets the same button. Jarvis's current 3 cards are all printings Anthony owns in some finish, so his count may read 0 — the empty state, not a fabricated list, is the truthful result.
**Binders tab removed everywhere**: the bottom nav's card-collection branch (binder/sealed/portfolio/social/settings views) still carried a "Binders" tab in v1.3.0 — removed; the bar is now grid-cols-6 in every variant (Home / Search / Sealed / Social / Portfolio / Settings, with Comics/Lego swapping Sealed for their own tab). No "Binders" label remains in any bottom menu (verified by source search: the only label: "Binders" occurrence was this one). The internal "binder" view is unchanged and is still reached directly by tapping the Lorcana (or other card-collection) tile on Home — no intermediate button. Version stays 1.4.0 for this release cycle (these additions ship with the Supabase wiring build).

### v1.10.0 — Shiny-style profile pages, achievements, Trade Match, big stepper targets, haptics (Nov 2, 2026)
**Profile pages (Anthony's Shiny profile screenshot, ~/workspace/user/files/9119_11_mwqe.jpg, as the layout reference)**: ONE shared layout (ProfileHeader + ProfileCollectionSection in App.tsx) for friend profiles in Social and his own profile (opened from "View my profile" in the full Collector ID view). Banner image, circular avatar overlapping bottom-left, back arrow, overflow menu (Copy Collector ID — no share button: the shell owns sharing), stats row Value • Collected • Trust Score, name + badge row, @handle, bio, "Joined <Month Year>", Trust Score + confirmed-connections chips, and the Collector ID kept small (code + mini QR). His bio is his own Shiny bio verbatim ("Just an average guy collecting cards letting his inner child let loose:)"); banners are existing owned assets (his: the Lorcana glance banner; Jarvis: the Lorcana collection tile). Items section: "All Collected Items" + total count, Viewing • group selector, Search items… bar, Sort (Total Value / Name / Quantity) + Filter (All Items / Cards / Sealed / Foils / Enchanteds) pills, two-column tiles (art, name, set line, finish in accent, "$ value • Qty N", and on friend tiles the red "Don't own" / green "You own ×N" overlap line). Deliberate honest omissions vs the Shiny reference: no Favorites stat (the app has no favorites — Trust Score stands in its slot), no per-item "Collected <date>" line (acquisition dates aren't tracked — never invented), condition line only where known (sealed = "Sealed"; card condition isn't tracked). Friend profiles still respect the share-my-collection switch (kept, with its "What Jarvis sees" preview, on Jarvis's Collection tab).
**Data**: three new actions, all reading Anthony's REAL data — getProfileItems (every owned printing expanded per finish with per-finish unit values from the Nov 1 2026 Shiny export finance data + the effective sealed shelf; name + collector-number search identical to main Search; display cap 240 with the true total always reported), getTradeMatchItems ("You Have, They Need": his owned cards whose name is absent from the friend's list), getProfileSummary (registry join date + Founder status, Trust Score, confirmed connections/trades counted from the Supabase connections/trust_events tables, total items, master sets completed under HIS pocket rule skipping Enchanted/Iconic rarities, Enchanted/Iconic ownership, sealed count).
**Achievements (earned-from-real-data only, same honesty rule as Trust)**: client/src/achievements.ts — 14 badges (Founder, First Steps, Century Club 100, Thousand Club 1,000, Five Figures 10,000, Master Setter, Completionist 3+ sets, Enchanted, Iconic, Sealed Keeper 5+, Slabbed, First Trade, Trusted Trader at Trust ≥ 10, Connected 5+ confirmed connections), each with a stroke icon, a how-earned line, and progress text for locked ones; evaluated purely from getProfileSummary-style inputs (his) or the friend's known figures (Jarvis). Badge row by the name (max 4 + count) opens the full earned/locked list.
**Trade Match (absorbs the "Cards They Have That I Need" button — it no longer exists as a button)**: a tab on friend profiles with a two-way toggle ("They Have, You Need" = friend's cards with viewer qty 0, client-filtered from the friend's resolved cards; "You Have, They Need" = getTradeMatchItems), each with a count line, name + collector-number search, and a set filter; honest empty states both ways.
**Stepper touch targets**: all +/− steppers rebuilt on one StepperButton — ~56px square hit area (-m-3 halo around a 28–32px layout footprint; sealed tiles use a 52px variant) with the visible circles unchanged, plus hapticTick on every press. The sealed tiles' stepper container gap widened (gap-1.5 → gap-6) so the grown halos never overlap; the v1.6.0 alignment geometry (full-height label, mt-auto row) is untouched.
**Haptics**: client/src/haptics.ts — Vibration API only, silent no-op where unsupported; hapticTick (10ms) on selections (bottom tabs via navigate, Search open, card/sealed openers, theme select, toggles/switches, steppers), hapticConfirm (28ms) on confirmations (friend found by code, price alert saved). Stored in localStorage vaultx-haptics with a Settings "Haptics" switch (default ON); turning it on fires one tick as a preview. Version 1.10.0 (minor: profiles + achievements + Trade Match).

### v1.11.0 — Marketplace phase 1: listings, Buy Now claims, offers, confirmed deals, mandatory blind feedback (Nov 2, 2026)
**Scope (Anthony: "Can we also create a marketplace?")**: phase 1 structure only — listings + offers, NO in-app payments (a later phase he approves explicitly, e.g. Stripe). The UI says so wherever money appears: Buy Now CLAIMS an item at the full asking price and notifies the seller (their Inbox); it never charges anything.
**Where it lives**: a fourth Social area ("Marketplace", beside Friends/Forums/Trades) plus a doorway row on Home under Collection Distribution (his "reachable from Home/Social"). Inside: Browse / Your Listings / Inbox tabs. Browse: name + collector-number search (same rule as main Search), type pills (All/Cards/Sealed/Comics), set filter, price/newest sort, two-column tiles (photo, name, set, finish • condition, big asking price, asking-vs-market chip, seller + Trust). Listing detail floats the photo on the Turntable (standing rule), asking price with the Buy Now button right beside it, Make an Offer as the secondary action, seller snippet (avatar, @handle, Trust Score, confirmed connections where known, View profile for Jarvis), and the honest how-buying-works note.
**Data (migration 0011)**: marketplace_listings / marketplace_offers / marketplace_feedback / marketplace_meta in the app DB. Listings are denormalized display rows (kind card/sealed/comic + refs); market value is re-resolved LIVE at read for cards (per-finish export finance) and sealed (effective shelf) so the fairness comparison is current; comics carry their logged value. Sell flow validates quantities server-side against the binder finishes / sealed shelf minus already-listed amounts. Cross-device sync of marketplace state is a later phase (with per-user Auth) — same staged path Social took; the registry/Trust layer it touches already lives on Supabase.
**Claims & deals**: a Buy Now claim flips the listing to "Sale pending" so nobody else can claim it; seller Accepts (deal starts, all other pending claims/offers on the item auto-decline) or Declines (claim frees the listing). Offers don't lock the item until accepted. Seller notifications = the Inbox (incoming claims/offers with the "@buyer wants to buy your [item] at your asking price of $X" wording, outgoing tracking, deals list). Completion is both-sides: each party confirms their side (the in-person QR step), and only then does the listing become Sold and the deal get written to the Supabase trust_events ledger (event_type "marketplace_sale_confirmed", points 2 per side, counterparty set; retried on later Inbox reads if the server was unreachable).
**Mandatory feedback (Anthony's forcing design)**: after completion, EACH side must leave thumbs up/down (optional one-tap tags — As described / Fair price / Friendly / Fast, or Not as described / Slow / Unresponsive — plus an optional comment) as the FINAL step; there is no skip. Bailing leaves the deal "Awaiting your feedback" with a persistent red banner, and the gate blocks that user from new claims, offers, accepts, and listings until they rate. Blind both ways: a side's rating/tags/comment are only revealed once BOTH are in. Safety valve: after 7 days unrated, the deal closes as neutral "No feedback" (no score impact) and the gate lifts.
**Test data**: seeded exactly once (seedMarketplace, marker-guarded, also the post-submit action) — the built-in test friend Jarvis's priced Friends-area cards listed at their market values, Near Mint (Elsa - Snow Queen Cold Foil ×1 at $2.00, Stitch - Rock Star Regular ×2 at $0.55), labeled "Test user" everywhere they appear. His third card (Mickey Mouse - Brave Little Tailor) is deliberately NOT listed: Anthony has never owned that printing, so it carries no price anywhere in his data, and inventing an asking price for it would be fabrication — it can be seeded later if a real price ever lands. Because Jarvis is a built-in test profile, his side of a deal (accept / confirm / feedback) is driven from clearly-labeled "(test)" controls so Anthony can walk a whole deal end-to-end; the server only honors those for MC-JARVIS. Anthony's own listings are only ever created by him in the Sell flow (his booster-box example works: sealed items list straight from the shelf). Version 1.11.0 (minor: Marketplace).
